<div class='question question'>

Students at Arlington Secondary School were polled regarding their favourite drinks.

![aldenham-school--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-23-00.png](/assets/aldenham-school--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-23-00.png "aldenham-school--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-23-00.png")

Given that there are $600$ students at the school, how many more preferred root beer than lemonade?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}                       &= \dfrac {1}{4} \times 600 - \dfrac {1}{6} \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= (\dfrac {1}{4} - \dfrac {1}{6}) \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= (\dfrac {1 \times 6}{4 \times 6} - \dfrac {1 \times 4}{6 \times 4} ) \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= (\dfrac {6}{24} - \dfrac {4}{24} ) \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2}{24}  \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 600}{24} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 12 \times 50}{2 \times 12} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{12} \times 50}{\cancel{2} \times \cancel{12}} \\\\
                                        &= 50
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}                       &= \dfrac {1}{4} \times 600 - \dfrac {1}{6} \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= (\dfrac {1}{4} - \dfrac {1}{6}) \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= (\dfrac {1 \times 6}{4 \times 6} - \dfrac {1 \times 4}{6 \times 4} ) \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= (\dfrac {6}{24} - \dfrac {4}{24} ) \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2}{24}  \times 600 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 600}{24} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 12 \times 50}{2 \times 12} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{12} \times 50}{\cancel{2} \times \cancel{12}} \\\\
                                        &= 50
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$50$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The number of boys who fail the test is $15$.

How many boys pass the test?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of boys $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {12} \times a            &= 15 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {15 \times 12} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 5 \times 12} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 5 \times 12} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                                   &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                   &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Students who passed}  &= a - 15 \\\\
                            &= 60 - 15 \\\\
                            &= 45
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$45$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of boys $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {12} \times a            &= 15 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {15 \times 12} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 5 \times 12} {3} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 5 \times 12} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                                   &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                   &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Students who passed}  &= a - 15 \\\\
                            &= 60 - 15 \\\\
                            &= 45
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$45$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Charlie says that more girls fail the test than boys. 

Explain why he might not be correct.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

The fraction of boys who failed the test is $\dfrac {3} {12}$ and the fraction of girls who failed the test is $\dfrac {5} {12}$.

Even though the fraction of girls who failed is greater, this doesnt necessarily mean that more girls failed.

This is because a fraction represents the proportion, rather than the exact number.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>The fraction of boys who failed the test is $\dfrac {3} {12}$ and the fraction of girls who failed the test is $\dfrac {5} {12}$.
<emptyline>
Even though the fraction of girls who failed is greater, this doesnt necessarily mean that more girls failed.
<emptyline>
This is because a fraction represents the proportion, rather than the exact number.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Students in $6\text{C}$ were asked about their favourite sports. The results are displayed in the pie chart below.

![aldenham-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-21-00.png](/assets/aldenham-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-21-00.png "aldenham-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-21-00.png")

$36$ students chose hockey.

How many students chose swimming?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of students in 6C $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{60}{360} \times a        &= 36 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{36 \times 360}{60} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{36 \times 60 \times 6}{60} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{36 \times \cancel{60} \times 6}{\cancel{60}} \\\\
a                               &= 36 \times 6 \\\\
a                               &= 216
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Students who chose swimming}  &= \dfrac{90}{360} \times a \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{90}{360} \times 216 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{90 \times 216}{360}  \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{90 \times 4 \times 54}{90 \times 4}  \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{\cancel{90}  \times \cancel{4} \times 54}{\cancel{90} \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
                                    &= 54
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$54$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of students in 6C $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{60}{360} \times a        &= 36 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{36 \times 360}{60} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{36 \times 60 \times 6}{60} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{36 \times \cancel{60} \times 6}{\cancel{60}} \\\\
a                               &= 36 \times 6 \\\\
a                               &= 216
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Students who chose swimming}  &= \dfrac{90}{360} \times a \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{90}{360} \times 216 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{90 \times 216}{360}  \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{90 \times 4 \times 54}{90 \times 4}  \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac{\cancel{90}  \times \cancel{4} \times 54}{\cancel{90} \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
                                    &= 54
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$54$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of people owned a black car?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fraction}    &= \dfrac {90} {360} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {90} {4 \times 90} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {90}} {4 \times \cancel {90}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fraction}    &= \dfrac {90} {360} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {90} {4 \times 90} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {90}} {4 \times \cancel {90}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Given that $5$ people owned a white car, how many people owned a silver car?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of people $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {30} {360} \times a          &= 5 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 360} {30} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times 30} {30} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times \cancel {30}} {\cancel {30}} \\\\
a                                   &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                   &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {People who own silver car}   &= \dfrac {180} {360} \times a \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {180} {360} \times 60 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {180 \times 60} {360} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {180 \times 2 \times 30} {2 \times 180} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {180} \times \cancel {2} \times 30} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {180}} \\\\
                                    &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of people $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {30} {360} \times a          &= 5 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 360} {30} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times 30} {30} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times \cancel {30}} {\cancel {30}} \\\\
a                                   &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                   &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {People who own silver car}   &= \dfrac {180} {360} \times a \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {180} {360} \times 60 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {180 \times 60} {360} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {180 \times 2 \times 30} {2 \times 180} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {180} \times \cancel {2} \times 30} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {180}} \\\\
                                    &= 30
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$30$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Tiles needed}        &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                            &= 16 \\\\
\text {Tiles used}          &= 1^2 + 2^2 + 3^2 \\\\
                            &= 1 + 4 + 9 \\\\
                            &= 14 \\\\
\text {Tiles Left}          &= 30 - 14 \\\\
                            &= 16
\end {aligned}
$

There are enough tiles to make the next square.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Yes

</div>

<div class='question question'>

She used the results to draw this pie chart.

![alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-24-00.png](/assets/alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-24-00.png "alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-24-00.png")

<div class='workings'>

</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of students said “Comedy”?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Fraction}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {90} {4 \times 90} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {90}} {4 \times \cancel {90}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Fraction}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {90} {4 \times 90} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {90}} {4 \times \cancel {90}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {4}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$20$ students said “Horror”.

Work out the total number of students that Betty asked.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of students $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {120} {360} \times a     &= 20 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 360} {120} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times 120} {120} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times \cancel {120}} {\cancel {120}} \\\\
a                               &= 20 \times 3 \\\\
a                               &= 60
\end {aligned}
$

Total number of students $= 60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of students $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {120} {360} \times a     &= 20 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 360} {120} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times 120} {120} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times \cancel {120}} {\cancel {120}} \\\\
a                               &= 20 \times 3 \\\\
a                               &= 60
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total number of students $= 60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Seven out of forty pupils said they watch the news on television. 
What angle would this be on a pie chart? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Angle}   &= \dfrac {7} {40} \times 360 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {7 \times 360} {40} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {7 \times 9 \times 40} {40} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {7 \times 9 \times \cancel {40}} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
                &= 7 \times 9 \\\\
                &= 63 \degree
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$63 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Angle}   &= \dfrac {7} {40} \times 360 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {7 \times 360} {40} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {7 \times 9 \times 40} {40} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {7 \times 9 \times \cancel {40}} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
                &= 7 \times 9 \\\\
                &= 63 \degree
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$63 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the area of the square $ABCD$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Side of the square}       &= \sqrt {(0 - 4) ^ 2 + (4 - 1) ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {(-4) ^ 2 + 3 ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {16 + 9} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {25} \\\\
                                &= 5 \\\\
\text{Area of the square}       &= \text{(Side of the square)} ^ 2 \\\\
                                &= 5 ^ 2 \\\\
                                &= 25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Side of the square}       &= \sqrt {(0 - 4) ^ 2 + (4 - 1) ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {(-4) ^ 2 + 3 ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {16 + 9} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {25} \\\\
                                &= 5 \\\\
\text{Area of the square}       &= \text{(Side of the square)} ^ 2 \\\\
                                &= 5 ^ 2 \\\\
                                &= 25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the area of the triangle formed by these three points?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Base of the triangle}     &= \sqrt {(-1 - 2) ^ 2 + (1 - 1) ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {(-3) ^ 2 + 0 ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {9 + 0} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {9} \\\\
                                &= 3 \\\\
\text{Height of the triangle}   &= 2 - 1 \\\\
                                &= 1 \\\\
\text{Area of the square}       &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text{(Base of the triangle)} \times \text{(Height of the triangle)} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 3 \times 1 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
                                &= 1.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                &= \sqrt {(-3) ^ 2 + 0 ^ 2} \\\\

                                &= \sqrt {9} \\\\

\text{Height of the triangle}   &= 2 - 1 \\\\
                                &= 1 \\\\
\text{Area of the square}       &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text{(Base of the triangle)} \times \text{(Height of the triangle)} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 3 \times 1 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
                                &= 1.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

North beat South in $1999, 2001$ and $2002$.

|   Year    | North        | South        | Difference    |
|:------:   |:--------:    |:--------:    |:----------------------:   |
| $1999$    |   $200$      |    $120$     |      $200 - 120 = 80$     |
| $2001$    |   $150$      |    $130$     |      $150 - 130 = 20$     |
| $2002$    |   $260$      |    $100$     |      $260 - 160 = 100$    |


Year $= 2002$

</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many children did Dr Patel see in July?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Children in July}    &= 240 - 80 \\\\
                            &= 160
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$160$

</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of the patients seen by Dr Patel in July were children?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Fraction}    &= \dfrac {240 - 80} {240} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {160} {240} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 80} {3 \times 80} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {80}} {3 \times \cancel {80}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2} {3}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {3}$

</div>
</div>

                    &= \dfrac {160} {240} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 80} {3 \times 80} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {80}} {3 \times \cancel {80}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2} {3}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {2} {3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

A square of side length $3 \ \text{cm}$ is divided into three
rectangles of equal size.

The middle rectangle is removed and replaced on the side of the 
original square to form an octagon as shown.

How many times bigger is the perimeter of the octagon than 
the perimeter of the original square?

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-35-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-35-subquestion-2-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2021--sample-paper-7/section-1-question-35-subquestion-2-00.png")

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of polygon} = \text{Sum of all sides}$


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



Plot the octagon and label the side length of all edges


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter of square}      &= 4 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= 12 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Perimeter of octagon}     &= 1 + 4 + 3 + 4 + 1 + 3 + 1 + 3 \\\\
                                &= 20 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Difference}               &= 20 - 12 \\\\
                                &= 8 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of houses}   &= \dfrac {6 + 6 + 1} {3 + 6 + 8 + 6 + 4 + 1} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {13} {28}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {13} {28}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

$

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

This formula tells you how tall a boy is likely to be when he grows up.

| Add the mother's and father's heights. Divide by $2$. Add $7 \ \text{cm}$ to the result. The boy is likely to be this height, plus or minus $10 \ \text{cm}$. |
|:-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------:|

Marc's mother is $168\ \text{cm}$ tall. His father is $194\ \text{cm}$ tall.

What is the greatest height Marc is likely to be when he grows up? 

Show your working.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Max height of Marc}           &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (168 + 194) + 7 + 10 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {168 + 194} {2} + 17  \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {362} {2} + 17 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 181} {2} + 17 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 181} {\cancel 2} + 17 \\\\
                                    &= 181 + 17 \\\\
                                    &= 198 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$191 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Max height of Marc}           &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (168 + 194) + 7 + 10 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {168 + 194} {2} + 17  \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {362} {2} + 17 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 181} {2} + 17 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 181} {\cancel 2} + 17 \\\\
                                    &= 181 + 17 \\\\
                                    &= 198 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$191 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

\dfrac {b} {2}              &= 50 \\\\

b                           &= 100
\end {aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

I asked $48$ year $7$ students what their favourite food was.

The results are shown in this pie chart.

![colfes-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper/section-1-question-18-00.png](/assets/colfes-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper/section-1-question-18-00.png "colfes-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper/section-1-question-18-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many year $7$s have chips as their favourite food?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Students who like chips}    &= \dfrac {180} {360} \times 48 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {180 \times 48} {360} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {180 \times 2 \times 24} {180 \times 2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {180} \times \cancel 2 \times 24} {\cancel {180} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
                                            &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {180} \times \cancel 2 \times 24} {\cancel {180} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
                                            &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

                                                    &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the area of the rectangle?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Length of rectangle}      &= \sqrt {(4 - 4) ^ 2 + (5 - 1) ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {0 ^ 2 + 4 ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {0 + 16} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {16} \\\\
                                &= 4 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Breadth of rectangle}     &= \sqrt {(-1 - 4) ^ 2 + (1 - 1) ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {(-5)) ^ 2 + 0 ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {25 + 0} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {25} \\\\
                                &= 5 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Area of rectangle}        &= \text{Length of rectangle} \times \text{Breadth of rectangle} \\\\
                                &= 4 \times 5 \\\\
                                &= 20 \ {\text{cm}} ^ 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Length of rectangle}      &= \sqrt {(4 - 4) ^ 2 + (5 - 1) ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {0 ^ 2 + 4 ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {0 + 16} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {16} \\\\
                                &= 4 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Breadth of rectangle}     &= \sqrt {(-1 - 4) ^ 2 + (1 - 1) ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {(-5)) ^ 2 + 0 ^ 2} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {25 + 0} \\\\
                                &= \sqrt {25} \\\\
                                &= 5 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Area of rectangle}        &= \text{Length of rectangle} \times \text{Breadth of rectangle} \\\\
                                &= 4 \times 5 \\\\
                                &= 20 \ {\text{cm}} ^ 2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total cousins} &= 3 \times 0 + 6 \times 1 + 8 \times 2 + 5 \times 3 + 3 \times 4 \\\\
                      &= 0 + 6 + 16 + 15 + 12 \\\\
                      &= 49
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$49$

</div>

<div class='workings'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



Show the coordinates of the origin and distance $2, 5, 6$ and $y$


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 : 5                                           &= 6 : y \\\\
\dfrac {2} {5}                                  &= \dfrac {6} {y} \\\\
2 \times y                                      &= 6 \times 5 \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 5} {2} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 5} {2} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3 \times 5} {\cancel 2} \\\\
y                                               &= 3 \times 5 \\\\
y                                               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

For how many hours is the tide above $6 \ \text{metres}?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of hours tide is above 6 metres}       &= 14 - 5 \\\\
                                                    &= 9 \ \text{hours}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9 \ \text{hours}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

\end{aligned}
$

</code></pre>
